Decision log (32)
  • 2026-10-31T16:59:46Z · D-GT23-C03-1: GT23 joint moments and powers were divided by another participant's body mass in most subjects, and the mass can change between trials of one participant. How should LocoHub release GT23 moments and powers? → Rescale each trial to its participant's own mass
  • 2026-10-31T06:18:26Z · D-UM21-T02-1: The UMich 2021 treadmill task intervals run on the belt-speed clock, which is 1.7 to 7 seconds off the motion data in some recordings. How should treadmill strides get task labels? → Exclude recordings that fail the clock check
  • 2026-10-31T06:18:26Z · D-CORE-04-1: May the sign detector change a channel's sign automatically when two tests with different failure modes agree (for example, markers plus knee range of motion, or lever arm plus late-stance timing)? → B, plus new cross-family tests
  • 2026-10-31T06:11:20Z · D-UM21-C03-1: Which sagittal sign rule should LocoHub adopt, with the person facing right and counterclockwise positive? → Segment angles counterclockwise, joint angles and moments positive in their named direction
  • 2026-10-30T17:24:31Z · D-UM21-C01-1: How should UMich 2021 treadmill thigh and shank angles be released? → Standard thigh/shank names from the same recipe
  • 2026-10-30T21:28:58Z · D-1: Which strides make up the first UMich 2021 treadmill release? → Task-window rule (8,626 strides)
  • 2026-10-30T21:28:58Z · D-UM21-C01-2: Should UMich 2021 treadmill ankle angle and hip/knee/ankle flexion moments become standard columns (moments only after the LocoHub moment sign rule is written)? → Ankle angle and flexion moments
  • 2026-10-30T17:34:34Z · D-UM21-C03-2: How should the broken right-ankle stretch in the UMich 2021 incline-10 recording of one participant be released? → Re-express the stretch with the logged rule; drop strides with sudden jumps
  • 2026-10-30T21:28:58Z · D-GT21-C01-1: How should Georgia Tech 2021 sideways ground force and centre of pressure be published? → Positive outward for each leg
  • 2026-10-30T21:35:09Z · D-GT21-C01-2: No pelvis forward/backward tilt derived from the authors' data matches marker tilt over treadmill strides. How should Georgia Tech 2021 pelvis tilt, and the thigh, shank, foot and trunk angles built on it, be handled? → Tilt from the pelvis markers directly
  • 2026-10-31T16:59:46Z · D-UM21-P01-1: In one UMich 2021 treadmill recording (AB04, level walking), Vicon computed the pelvis facing backwards, so its stored pelvis tilt has the wrong sign, and so do the thigh and shank angles built from it. How should that recording's pelvis-dependent columns be released? → Keep AB04 i0 with pelvis tilt sign-flipped from marker evidence; thigh and shank from it
  • 2026-10-31T16:59:46Z · D-GT21-S01-1: Six of the 22 Georgia Tech 2021 participants are missing here. Their checksummed publisher archives (2021) lack the static-pose trials and static-relative joint angles that our converter reads; the authors' 2024 copy, which the other 16 match the 2024 content of, has them but no publisher checksums. Where should the six come from? → Authors' 2024 copy
  • 2026-10-31T17:42:39Z · D-HIST-01-1: Eleven historical studies came from AddBiomechanics. Their source files were not found in the searched folders, and the old LocoHub files cannot be traced to any source file. Five also have openly licensed original data from the publishers. From which source, if any, should LocoHub rebuild them? → Publisher originals where open, AddBiomechanics otherwise
  • 2026-10-31T18:51:54Z · D-HIST-03-1: Three historical studies have no complete original data outside AddBiomechanics: Falisse (none found), Tan running (only the AddBiomechanics copy), and Tan walking (the authors share only cut-out right-foot stance steps). AddBiomechanics force data turned out to be measured plate forces, not predictions. What should LocoHub do with these three? → AddBiomechanics for all three
  • 2026-10-31T22:53:28Z · D-GT23-C04-1: Only some GT23 trials have a clearly identified divisor. Which GT23 trials should carry moments and powers rescaled to the participant's own mass? Trials not rescaled keep kinematics and forces; their moments and powers are withheld. → Deferred
  • 2026-10-31T22:33:48Z · D-HIST-04-1: Each AddBiomechanics frame holds the raw sensor data plus three processed versions of the motion (marker fit, filtered, and force-matched). The current converter writes all four as separate rows. How many rows per frame should LocoHub write for Falisse and both Tan studies? → One row per frame from the force-matched (dynamics) result
  • 2026-10-31T22:33:48Z · D-HIST-04-2: Eight of the eleven AddBiomechanics Falisse participants very likely are the volunteers of the cited 2017 paper, but three extra men are unexplained, and the files hold only 3 of the paper's 6 motions (gait, sit-to-stand, stair ascent; squat, stair descent and squat jump are absent). Should LocoHub contact the AddBiomechanics team or the KU Leuven authors to ask which participants are the paper's volunteers, who the other three are, and which protocol the trials followed? → No, do not contact
  • 2026-11-02T17:00:00Z · D-GT23-C05-1: Rescaling GT23 moments to each participant's own mass lowers the error in only some trials. Which GT23 trials should carry moments and powers? Trials left out keep kinematics and forces; their moments and powers are withheld. → No rescaling: release every GT23 trial's moments and powers exactly as published (original weight), with the divisor mismatch documented
  • 2026-11-02T17:00:00Z · D-GT24-C01-1: GT24 exoskeleton torques and powers were divided exactly by the person-plus-exoskeleton (worn) mass in all 2,421 recordings. The net joint moments match the same worn mass only within a few percent, from a lever-arm check against the force plates. Which mass should LocoHub's standard GT24 moment, power and force columns be per kg of? → Worn mass (person + exoskeleton)
  • 2026-11-02T17:00:00Z · D-GT23-C01-1: Our sign rule defines forward (+x) as the walking direction. In GaTech 2023, 15 of 26 activities have no single forward walk (backward walking, side shuffle, squats, sit-to-stand, lifts, jumps, lunges, turns, cutting, meander and others). What should forward mean there for forward and sideways ground force and centre of pressure? → Forward walking only
  • 2026-11-03T12:00:00Z · D-UM21-Q01-1: What should the first UMich 2021 treadmill release check for plausibility? → Structure plus coarse physical limits
  • 2026-11-03T12:00:00Z · D-UM21-T04-1: When a channel's sign is settled on one leg but unsure on the other, what is published? → Copy the settled leg's sign to the other leg
  • 2026-11-03T12:00:00Z · D-UM21-T04-2: What happens to the 15 strides outside the sign check's 50–250 frame stride rule? → Leave them out of the release with a listed reason
  • 2026-11-03T12:00:00Z · D-UM21-T04-3: Power is held in most recordings because only one evidence family agrees. What is published? → Set power's sign so power = published moment × joint speed
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-GT23-C05-2: Should GT23 moments and powers be rescaled to each person's own mass in the recordings where the mass the authors used is known to be another person's? This replaces the earlier answer of no rescaling. → fix the 52
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-GT23-C05-3: José said to fix the 52. Of those 52 recordings, only 7 have the mass the authors used worked out exactly; for the other 45 it was estimated from force plates and matched to the nearest recorded mass. Should all 52 be rescaled, or only the 7? → Rescale all 52
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-GT23-C01-2: Should GT23 strides come from the authors' event windows, or from LocoHub's own segmentation scripts? → We can totally segment ourselves. Instead of using their segmentation, use our segmentation scripts and validate against their labeled strides.
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-REF-1: What defines the zero of joint angles in every dataset? → joint angles are differences of link angles. Defining link angles therefore defines joint angles.
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-REF-2: Where is the centre-of-pressure origin, in every dataset? → The origin should rotate with the foot. Ie it should be 100mm from the ankle center pointing forward in the ankle-foot frame.
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-GT21-REB-1: The Georgia Tech 2021 markers have no hip marker and only outer (lateral) knee and ankle markers. How should the thigh and shank axes, the ankle centre and the foot direction be found? → Authors' scaled model locates the centres: fit its marker positions to the measured markers each frame (no joint angles taken from the model)
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-UM21-S02-1: The UMich 2021 treadmill plate position is not recorded; it can only be fitted from the data. Should the centre of pressure built on that fitted position be published? → Publish, labelled as an estimated plate position
  • 2026-11-23T00:00:00Z · D-DOC-06-1: Which datasets should the public site show? → José, 23 November 2026: "remove some of the old datasets ... only keep the three that we are working with now. That way we have the updated 'stuff that we fixed' pages." Refinement: the other datasets still appear in the catalog, as plain text, in a closed collapsible section titled for later; their detail pages are not built.

Release checks

These checks describe the data, separately from task progress. Unreviewed: evidence not checked. Partial: some evidence exists, but the check remains open. Verified: the recorded check is satisfied. Not applicable: the check does not apply. An omitted status is shown as unreviewed.

Evidence needed for each release check

Sources and coverage

Do we know the exact original version and all source scope?

  • Version, license and source identity recorded.
  • All participants, recordings and observation types inventoried; gaps are explicit.

Source meaning

Are clocks, sides, units, signs and frames supported by source evidence?

  • Every claimed mapping has source evidence.
  • Unknown timing, mass, anatomy and measurements remain explicit.

Representative pilot

Does an independently checked pilot support the planned mapping?

  • Known reference cases cover the changed behavior and relevant failure cases.
  • Actual persisted pilot values, identities and missingness match independently read sources.

Complete source comparison

Has the complete declared export been reconciled to its original sources?

  • All exported observations and claimed signals checked over their declared scope.
  • Every source observation has a converted, excluded, missing or failed disposition.

Quality and limitations

Do diagnostics and profiles describe what is known without hiding missing evidence?

  • One assessment records passed, failed, missing, unchecked and invalid results.
  • Any profile has justified population, task and observation applicability; source quality limitations remain visible.

Release review

Do exact artifacts, evidence and researcher-facing claims agree?

  • Full and filtered artifacts share source lineage and exclusions.
  • Immutable manifest, hashes, source comparison evidence and actual review decision reconciled.
  • Downloads and previews match the reviewed artifact or remain explicitly legacy/unreviewed.

UM21-T01 — Define the first treadmill release scope and membership · Done

Latest handoff

Field-by-field release policy for the UMich 2021 treadmill signals, with exact author and Vicon evidence plus a bounded marker/kinetic probe (4 random recordings, seed 20261028, plus the AB02/i5 lead and 2 anomaly recordings; an independent verifier reproduced it on 5 disjoint recordings, seed 4242). Canonical: pelvis tilt, hip flexion and knee flexion (Vicon text: PSIS above ASIS positive; authors: flexion and dorsiflexion positive; pelvis matches marker tilt with median error within 0.1 deg in all 10 trials of both samples). Derived estimate: hip/knee velocities. Source-only: powers (source power has the opposite sign of internal moment x joint speed in 60 of 60 joint-sides; slope -0.71 to -1.02), joint forces, non-sagittal components, foot progression, and force plates (newtons and millimetres, vs README N/kg and metres; walking direction flips between trials). The current pelvis + hip thigh/shank values are wrong by about twice the pelvic tilt against markers; the author hip - pelvis recipe tracks them. They are withheld in every option. Two decisions are open: thigh/shank release form, and whether ankle angle and sagittal moments become canonical. The AB07 incline-10 right ankle run and the AB08 level left excursion (screen-dependent: 9 frames at 45 deg, 8 at 60, 2 at 90) stay source-only and unshifted; canonical ankle stays missing for both whole recording-sides. The coordinator lead (AB02/i5 hip never in extension) is explained by Plug-in Gait hip being relative to an anteriorly tilted pelvis (mean tilt 18.8 deg). It is not a sign error, and it supports hip - pelvis.

Latest handoff

Yes, the normalized and streaming pelvis fields describe the same physical stride. All 7,224 normalized strides in the 44 T03 treadmill recordings bind exactly (explicit StrideDetails side and frames, consecutive same-side heel strikes, duration = end - start; no concatenation order used). For every one, all three normalized pelvis components equal the same-side streaming pelvis resampled to the same 150 points (largest difference 2.8e-14 deg, no missing-value or zero-fill differences). The opposite-side streaming pelvis matches tilt but fails obliquity and rotation in all 7,224 (left and right tilt are identical, so tilt cannot tell them apart); a sign-flipped pelvis fails everywhere. A same-side hip control matches 7,224/7,224 with the 1-based frame base and 0 with a one-frame shift. AB04/i0 normalized carries the same reversed pelvis as streaming, so the two are not independent evidence there. No planar formula adopted; canonical 3D unapproved; not scientific approval.

Latest handoff

Proposed a LocoHub sagittal frame (x walking direction, y up, z = person's right side out of the page, counterclockwise positive) and derived each signal's sign. Pure right-hand rule conflicts with clinical names in three places: pelvis anterior tilt, knee flexion angle and knee flexion moment (all negative under pure RHR). Every UM21 angle and moment channel was fitted (component x sign x offset) against independent evidence on 8 recordings, 16 legs (seed 20261030: AB07/i5, AB03/i5, AB08/d10, AB05/d5; disjoint seed 20261031: AB05/d10, AB04/d5, AB06/d5, AB01/i5). Best transform is component 1 with the published sign under the clinical convention for pelvis, hip, knee and ankle angles (best of 6 candidates in 16/16 legs each; median absolute residual against marker angles 0.03-0.14 deg pelvis, 0.65-2.6 hip, 0.97-3.3 knee, 0.41-1.5 ankle; opposite sign 1.8-31 deg). Under pure RHR the same component wins with sign -1 for pelvis and knee. Knee and ankle moments: component 1 wins against force-plate lever-arm moments in 15/16 and 14/16 legs; the published sign beats the flipped sign in 16/16 for both (knee needs x -1 under pure RHR); slope 0.75-0.91, so N*m/kg as stored. Hip moment is weak (component 1 wins in 8/16; published sign better in 14/16). New finding: power is stored in two layouts. In 42 recordings, components 1-3 are filled and component 1 = -(moment x joint speed). In 8 recordings (AB03/i10, AB04/d10, AB04/d5, AB04/i5, AB05/d10, AB05/d5, AB05/i0, AB05/i10), components 1-2 are zero and component 3 = +(moment x joint speed). Both layouts agree with force-plate moment x marker joint speed after that transform (power is invariant to the convention choice). Ankle: the AB07/i10 right run (1-based frames 11704-17134, 5429 frames, 34 strides) is the alternate Cardan solution (c1+180, 180-c2, c3+180) of a continuous ankle orientation. After re-expression, inside p1-p99 is [-16.6, 27.0], [1.1, 6.1], [-24.2, -3.4] deg vs outside [-15.9, 27.8], [0.5, 6.3], [-24.9, -0.5], and the frame-to-frame orientation step inside the run is 0.51 deg median, 2.43 deg at the 95th percentile (outside 0.64 and 2.77). The matrix identity (raw and re-expressed triples give the same rotation, 1.3e-15) holds for any triple; it checks the rule, it is not evidence. The run is not all clean: 13 frames change orientation by 20-171 deg in one frame (1-based clusters 11704-11713, 12704-12705, 14931-14932, 14973, 14986-14991). These are not alternate solutions; 29 of 31 inside strides are free of them. Census of all 500 joint-angle channels: alternate-branch frames in 3 ankle channels only (the AB07/i10 R episode plus AB05/d10 L 5 frames and AB06/d5 R 8 frames, which are glitches, not branch switches) and 21 FootProgress channels (left source-only). Also found: exact (0,0,0) fill in 241 of 500 angle channels, 23,386 frames between first and last heel strike in 87 channels.

Checks completed

  • Publisher scripts re-read. Citations corrected: C3DtoMOT 74, 83-85 and createExternalLoads 91-96. Added C3DtoMOT 24, 44-45, 58-78 and 98: only the local Fz/Mx/My used for COP are filtered (default 15 Hz, filtfilt). Local COP is set to the plate origin when local Fz > -10 N. The exported F and M are unfiltered btk global wrenches. The adapter's 0.2 BW mask removes those COP samples.
  • Pelvis composition test on all 8 sampled recordings (2 per mode). Marker frame: x = mean ASIS - mean PSIS, z = R - L ASIS orthogonalized. One constant Procrustes offset was fitted against Rz(tilt) Rx(list) Ry(rotation) from source IK, then decomposed in the same order. Wrapped, median-centred RMS: rotation 0.47-1.33 deg, tilt 0.9-2.7 deg, list 0.85-1.67 deg. Without the offset, tilt and list disagreed on overground recordings (list RMS 14-17 deg), because the marker-to-model offset projects with a heading-dependent sign. A 3-parameter constant fit cannot create time-varying agreement or choose a sign.
  • Sheets rebuilt. Neutral option order is stated in the builder: most sideways values published first, ties broken by less adapter work. The recommended option is now last (C). The chip that repeated the recommendation's reasoning was removed. 'Cost: none' became 'Adapter work: rename columns'. The X-direction diagram was split into +X (forward) and -X (backward) panels. The README is wrong for the ankle; the data are dorsiflexion-positive and the prior audits' negation proposal is withdrawn. Seed 20261030 drew 6 subjects across both data phases (Phase1And2 BT10 decline 10, BT07 level 1.2 m/s off, BT11 decline 5; Phase3 BT02 level 2.0, BT24 incline 5, BT20 incline 5), 12 legs, 231 stances. (2) Lever-arm ankle moment (generic gait2392 talus offset): source ankle_angle_<side>_moment / dorsiflexion-positive internal estimate = 0.96-1.11 in 12/12 legs (correlation 0.987-0.999), same sign in 231/231 stance means, positive for every one of 9 ankle positions (0.77-1.49). (3) Level/uphill push-off net work positive in 154/154 stances (publisher power and own moment x d(angle)/dt identical); since sign(work) = moment sign x angle sign, the angle is dorsiflexion-positive too. Declines are not diagnostic (32/77 positive) and are excluded from that test. (4) Angle falls from 70 % stance to plate toe-off in 154/154 level/uphill stances; toe walking (measured CoP 12-17 cm further forward than heel walking) gives a lower ankle angle in 12/12 legs (BT02 L by 0.08 deg, BT11 R by 0.53 deg). (5) moment_filt_bio equals moment_filt for the ankle exactly and ankle power_exo is 0 (no ankle actuator). Exo encoder knee angle correlates +0.93 to +0.99 with OpenSim knee_angle and exo hip -0.90 to -0.99 with hip_flexion: the README hip/knee and exo statements are right; only its ankle statement is wrong. Moment/mass slope about 1 with README "w/ exo" masses is consistent with N*m/kg.

CORE-04 — Build a statistical sign and offset detector for every dataset · Done

Question: Can one dataset-agnostic tool infer each channel's sign convention and flag offsets, so conversion needs no per-dataset sign code?

Scope: Detector over standardized-candidate columns using independent physical relations: power = moment x joint speed, segment chain (thigh = pelvis + hip under the adopted counterclockwise rule), stance plantarflexor moment from force plates, swing knee flexion, marker angles where present. Outputs a per-channel sign with confidence and an offset estimate. Signs may be applied when the evidence is unambiguous and logged; offsets are flagged for review, never applied automatically (AGENTS.md: no empirical offsets).

Next action: Done. Detector (build ed52fc49) piloted on UM21, GT23, GT24 and GT21 with 0 wrong signs; every pilot independently re-run on fresh subjects. Converter integration is CORE-11.

Depends on: UM21-C03 · Resources: Numerical

Inputs

  • docs/quality/audits/sign-conventions-source-survey.json

Latest handoff

GT23 published moments (N*m/kg) were not divided by the participant's own mass in most subjects, and the divisor can change between trials of one subject. Evidence 1 (exact): RawData/<subj>/MotionFile_EH/inverse_dynamics.sto (local N*m inverse dynamics, NOT in the publisher archive directory, provenance unknown) equals the published normal_walk_1 moments times a constant (corr 1.0; max residual 0.0-0.03 N*m in AB01/05/06/08/10/12, 0.2-0.4 AB09, 3.5-4.7 AB13 on p99 100-260 N*m) for 8 subjects. Divisors: AB01 78.9 (own), AB05 78.9 (own 71.5), AB06 71.5 (own 79.1), AB08 62.3 (own 87.6), AB09 87.57 (own 84.1), AB10 84.1 (own 67.5), AB12 65.1 (own 64.0), AB13 64.0 (own 67.6). Evidence 2 (independent, mass-free): force-plate lever-arm moment in the calcn frame with joint centres/axes from each trial's own scaled model (Statics.csv; foot is unscaled in every used model, so ankle = generic 0.04877/0.04195 m; knee uses the subject's tibia length and Joint_Angle ankle/subtalar angles). The lever-arm bias (0.9942, robust sd 0.015 log) was calibrated on 5 of the 8 re-run trials (AB01, AB06, AB09, AB12, AB13); out of sample it held on 3 of 3 (AB05 2.1 %, AB08 1.3 %, AB10 4.9 %, the last at the band edge). It tracks the exact divisor, not the README mass (fig 3). Lever-arm level-walk divisors for the other subjects: AB02 82.1 (own 82.2), AB03 82.6 (own 113.5; AB02's 82.2), AB07 76.6 (own 62.3; AB06's 79.1), AB11 67.3 (own 65.1 / AB10's 67.5, ambiguous). README masses are true body masses (force plates within 1 % in 12/12; model masses equal README). Established counts: the level-walk divisor is not the subject's own mass in 9 of 12 subjects (AB03, AB05, AB06, AB07, AB08, AB09, AB10, AB12, AB13). In 7 of them a re-run fixes it at another subject's README table mass (AB05 78.9 = AB01; AB06 71.5; AB08 62.3; AB10 84.1; AB12 65.1 exact, residual 0.0-0.03 N*m; AB09 87.57 and AB13 64.0 near-exact, residual 0.2-0.4 and 3.5-4.7 N*m). In 6 of these it is the previous subject in README order (AB06, AB08, AB09, AB10, AB12, AB13); AB05's level walk uses AB01's mass instead. AB03 and AB07 are force-plate only and not unique: AB03 82.6 fits 82.2 (AB02) or 84.1 (AB09), and within this worker's +/-4.9 % band also 78.9 and 79.1; AB07 76.6 fits 79.1 (AB06) or 78.9 (AB01). Both clearly exclude their own mass (113.5, 62.3). AB01 is correct (own 78.9, exact); AB02 and AB11 cannot be told apart from own vs another mass at 5 %. AB05 incline_walk_1, incline_walk_2, turn_and_step_1 and dynamic_walk_1 use about 113-118 kg (consistent with AB03's 113.5; ankle and knee agree). The exact divisors are the one-decimal table values (AB06 71.5 gives 0.0 N*m residual; AB05's model mass 71.541 gives 0.09-0.14), consistent with a table lookup offset; the mechanism is not established. Published values are 0.61-1.46 times the correctly normalized per-kg value over 52 trials with ankle corr >= 0.98 (subject level-walk factors: AB03 1.375, AB05 0.906 [0.63 in incline/turn/dynamic], AB06 1.106, AB07 0.814, AB08 1.406, AB09 0.960, AB10 0.803, AB12 0.983, AB13 1.056). Trial outliers confirmed by ankle and knee besides AB05: AB12 cutting_1 (~82.6 kg vs 65.1) and AB13 tire_run_1 (~77 kg vs 64.0); cause not identified. Curb trials give unreliable lever estimates (ankle corr < 0.98 in 6 of 9). Prior C02 findings explained: AB03 78-85 kg is consistent with AB02's 82.2 kg but not unique (not the AB02 model file: its tibia lengths differ from AB02's own model, and the file is unused per Statics.csv); AB05 incline_walk_2 114-120 kg is consistent with AB03's 113.5 kg; AB10 1.33x = 84.1/67.5 = 1.25 (exact); AB08 0.73x = 62.3/87.6 = 0.71 (exact). Nothing rescaled or excluded. Handling drafted as decision D-GT23-C03-1.

DOC-02 — Write the adopted LocoHub sagittal sign rule into the reference · Done

Question: Does the public reference state the adopted sign and frame rules exactly?

Scope: Document decisions D-UM21-C03-1 (C: counterclockwise for everything, gait2392: hip flexion +, knee flexion -, ankle dorsiflexion +, pelvis anterior tilt -; moments internal with the same sign as their angle; power = moment x joint speed) and D-GT21-C01-1 (B: sideways GRF/CoP positive outward per leg). CORE-05 — Make release publishing work on the Windows-mounted repo drive · Done

Question: Can the release builder publish a new bundle on /mnt/s without overwriting anything?

Scope: The no-overwrite publish rename fails with EINVAL on the drvfs mount (/mnt/s) but works on Linux filesystems (found in CORE-02, gap G1). Add a safe fallback (e.g., exclusive mkdir plus per-file no-clobber moves with a lock and rollback) or document and enforce building on a Linux path then copying with verification.

Next action: Done. Publishing on /mnt/s uses an exclusive-mkdir + hard-link fallback with RELEASE_INCOMPLETE.txt marker; never overwrites. Follow-ups V1, V2 in CORE-06.

Depends on: CORE-02 · Resources: Fixture

Inputs

  • docs/quality/audits/core02-release-lineage.json
  • docs/maintainers/handoffs/CORE-02-verify.json

Deliverables

  • Publish path that works on /mnt/s and refuses any overwrite
  • Fixture tests on both a Linux tmp path and /mnt/s

Acceptance checks

  • Existing destination never overwritten on either filesystem
  • Interrupted publish leaves no partial bundle or a clearly marked incomplete one
  • No research data read

Evidence

  • docs/maintainers/handoffs/CORE-05.json
  • docs/maintainers/handoffs/CORE-05-verify.json

Latest handoff

G1 reproduced on /mnt/s (9p, aname=drvfs): renameat2(RENAME_NOREPLACE) returns EINVAL for a new destination; hard links, O_EXCL and mkdir EEXIST work there. _publish_directory still tries the atomic no-replace rename first. It falls back only on EINVAL, ENOSYS or EOPNOTSUPP, or when libc has no renameat2 (previously a ReleaseError). The fallback runs under the existing sibling lock and, before touching the destination, requires the staged files to equal manifest.json artifacts plus manifest.sha256. It then makes the destination with exclusive os.mkdir and writes RELEASE_INCOMPLETE.txt (O_EXCL) first. Each file is placed by os.link, or by an O_EXCL-create copy with fsync when the filesystem lacks hard links; EEXIST always propagates, so no file is ever replaced. manifest.json and manifest.sha256 go last. It then checks that the directory holds exactly the expected entries and that every hash matches, and removes the marker last. On any exception, including KeyboardInterrupt, it removes the files it placed. It removes the marker and the directory only if nothing foreign was added; otherwise the marker stays. A hard kill leaves the marker and the lock. The Windows-native branch and the atomic Linux path are unchanged.

Latest handoff

Placeholders: added -nan, <missing>, <undefined>, n/a, #n/a; matching is case-insensitive after NFKC (full-width) folding and zero-width removal; NA stays valid. Composite IDs are rejected when a part split on / \ : | ; , is a NaN-family or MATLAB/CSV marker (nan, -nan, <na>, <missing>, <undefined>, n/a, #n/a). none/null/nat parts are kept because a path part such as 'none' can be a real condition label. The check runs on unique values only. Each affected observation gets its own invalid_identity issue naming it. A separate file-level placeholder_identity_in_file issue keeps every observation in that file ineligible, because a detached placeholder row may belong to any observation; this preserves the verifier's fail-closed test. A stringified missing task_id is invalid_metadata. profile_applicability_gaps(config) lists tasks of a reviewed/approved profile with no nonempty applicability block. G7: _nonempty_strings checks unique values as plain objects and groupby uses observed=True, so single- and multi-category identities are assessed like strings with no FutureWarning.

HIST-06 — Map the AddBiomechanics Falisse and Tan frames to LocoHub conventions, including Tan task labels · Done

Question: How do the 33 HIST-05 frames files map to LocoHub task labels, joint names, signs and normalization, without labelling non-protocol trials?

Scope: Inputs are the HIST-05 frames files (one row per frame, dynamics pass, source names). (1) MAP-TAN-TASK: any Tan trial (running or walking) whose original name does not match the study's protocol pattern (running {nike|mini}_{SR|SI|baseline}_{24|28}; walking baseline|fpa|step_width|trunk_sway) gets no task label. Tan walking task family (level_walking versus dynamic_walk) and the Falisse task mapping remain conventions.

Working rules

Choosing work

  • Use next_task_id as the current starting point. Reassess it after each handoff rather than launching a long chain of dependent jobs.
  • Prioritize restoring a reviewed single-pilot execution path, then UMich 2021 treadmill evidence and GT21 source conventions and coverage.
  • Read-only source investigations can proceed when their own inputs and dependencies permit, even if a numerical task is blocked.
  • Keep other active and historical datasets in scope. Add new datasets sequentially after full reviewed disposition of the current candidate; first-pair approval permits only expansion of that candidate.
  • The backlog is a reviewed snapshot. Check source receipts and resource conditions at execution time; never infer current memory or worker state from this page.

Leaving a handoff

  • Ask José only for scientific judgments, scope/priority, and releases: add a decision (question, options, recommended, why) to decisions; it renders under Needs José. Everything else the agent decides and logs.
  • Read this backlog, the task inputs and current evidence before starting. Chat history is supporting context, not the authoritative handoff.
  • Continue one ready task per agent session. Record its ID and start time, and update its state when the work changes.
  • Keep the full declared source scope. Splitting a task or publishing a limited artifact does not complete missing participants, recordings or signals.
  • A job definition or saved waiting status is not proof of a live process. Verify the exact process identity; preserve terminal receipts and plan a fresh reviewed attempt.
  • Use one numerical worker, low process and I/O priority, bounded reads and the existing admission limits. Coordinate native MATLAB work before launch.
  • Keep source files, prior outputs and frozen job inputs immutable. Changed sources or code require fresh bindings and a new output identity.
  • At handoff record changed files, exact checks and their coverage, output evidence, remaining questions, the next action and any condition needed to unblock it.
  • Move completed deliverables to review; a second agent marks done only when every acceptance criterion is evidenced; synthetic tests, source hashes or queue completion alone do not approve a scientific conversion.
  • Independent source checks must avoid reusing the converter assumption being tested. Report a source mismatch or unresolved convention instead of fitting signs or ranges to make it pass.
  • Record excluded and unavailable observations or signals explicitly. No profile match means unchecked; do not tune ranges to obtain a target pass rate.
  • The release gate records the actual reviewer and exact artifact identity. Publishing data still follows the user-requested destination and workflow.

Task states

Ready
Dependencies are satisfied; the next action can be taken within its stated resource limits.
Working
An agent is working on this task; verify the handoff and any process identity before continuing it.
Review
Deliverables are ready for verification by a second, independent agent against the acceptance criteria.
Blocked
A named dependency, missing source, decision, or resource condition prevents the next action.
Done
A verifying agent checked every acceptance criterion against recorded evidence; this does not approve the whole dataset.

Stages

Source inventory
Identify the original files, versions, participants, observations, licenses and missing scope.
Conventions
Establish timing, physical side, units, signs, frames and normalization from source evidence.
Conversion
Implement the documented source mapping and preserve unavailable or unresolved data.
Pilot
Compare a small representative conversion with independently read source values.
Source readback
Reconcile every claimed output and excluded source observation over the complete declared scope.
Quality
Review missingness, signal diagnostics and any population/task-specific profile with explicit limitations.
Release
Reconcile immutable local artifacts, source lineage, exclusions, checks and review decisions.
Operations
Review safe execution and current process state under the shared-host limits.
Documentation
Keep researcher-facing descriptions, previews and quality claims aligned with reviewed evidence.
